Lemma 0.

(1)

If
\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

then
\mathcal{U}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}/t]\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

then
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of

\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

•

US:Corecursive
.
By inversion on the rule.

•

US:Subtype
.
Then
\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}/t]\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
.

•

US:Commutative
.
Then
\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}/t]\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
Apply
US:Commutative
.

(2)

By induction on the derivation of

\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.

•

UT:ProdLeft
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a_{1}}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{a_{2}}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{5}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{a_{2}}
.
By Lemma
1
,

a_{2}=\blacksquare
.
By Lemma
1
,
there exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{5}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{5}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By
US:SplitRight
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a_{1}}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}
.
By
UT:PairLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a_{1}}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{5}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}
.
By
UT:PairLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{5}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
four times.

•

UT:ProdLeft
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

UT:Prod
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a_{1}}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{a_{2}}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime a_{1}}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime a_{2}}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{4}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
1
, there are three cases:

(a)

a_{1}=\blacksquare
and
a_{2}=\square
.
By Lemma
1
,
there exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
By
UT:Pair
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}
.
By
UT:Pair
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
four times.

(b)

a_{1}=\square
and
a_{2}=\blacksquare
. By symmetry with the previous case.

(c)

a_{1}=a_{2}=\blacksquare
.
By Lemma
1
, there are seven cases:

(i)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By
US:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
By
UT:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}
.
By
UT:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
twice.

(ii)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(iii)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
By
UT:Pair
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}
.
By
UT:Pair
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
four times.

(iv)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(v)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(vi)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(vii)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitBoth
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
twice.

•

UT:Corec1
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}/t]
.
Apply US:Recursive.

•

UT:Corec2
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}/t]

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
.
By part
1
.

•

UT:Transitive
.
Then
\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction.

∎

Lemma 0.

(1)

If
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
then:

(a)

If
\mathcal{U}_{1}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

then there exists a
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

such that
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{U}_{2}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime}

then there exists a
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

such that
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}
.

(2)

If
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
then:

(a)

If
\Omega_{1}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}

then there exists an
\Omega^{\prime}

such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
.

(b)

If
\Omega_{2}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}

then there exists an
\Omega^{\prime}

such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}
.

(3)

If
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega_{2}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}

then there exists an
\Omega^{\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}

such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

•

US:Prod
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.

(a)

If
\mathcal{U}_{1}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
by Lemma
1
,
there exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
By
US:Subtype
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
By
UT:ProdLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
twice.

(b)

If
\mathcal{U}_{2}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
,
by symmetry with the previous case.

•

US:SplitLeft
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.

(a)

If
\mathcal{U}_{1}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
there are two cases:

(i)

a=\square
.
By Lemma
1
,
there exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
By
US:Subtype
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
.

(ii)

a=\blacksquare
.
By Lemma
1
, there are seven cases:

(A)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
By
UT:ProdLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
By two applications of
US:Subtype
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
.

(B)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(C)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
By
US:Subtype
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
.

(D)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(E)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.

,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}

UT:ProdLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}

US:Subtype
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
.

(F)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}

and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

By symmetry with the previous case.

(G)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}

there exists

,

,

,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}

,

US:Subtype
twice.

(b)

If

by symmetry with the previous case when

US:SplitRight
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

US:SplitBoth
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}

(a)

If
\mathcal{U}_{1}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
by Lemma
1
, there are seven cases:

(i)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

,

\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
.
By

,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}
.

,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}

US:Subtype
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

,

\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}

,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\square}
.
By
US:Commutative
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

UT:ProdRight
,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}
.
Apply
US:Subtype
.

(ii)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(iii)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}

and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}

,

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\square}

∎

With the help of Lemmas
4
and
5
, Lemma
6
proves that
\Omega
context splitting maintains weakening in VS typing: if
\Omega
splits into
\Omega^{\prime}
and
U
is well-typed under
\Omega^{\prime}
, then
U
is also well-typed under
\Omega
. The notable complication is a result of rule
OM:VarTypeSplit
: a variable
u
may be mapped to
\mathcal{U}
in
\Omega
while being mapped to
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
in
\Omega^{\prime}
if
\mathcal{U}
splits into
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. In order for
\Omega^{\prime}
to be weakened to
\Omega
, it must be valid for
u
to have type
\mathcal{U}
when it was expected to have type
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
; in other words,
\mathcal{U}
must be a subtype of
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. This is accomplished by Lemma
4
, which shows that VS types always split into supertypes. Lemma
5
acts as intermidiate between
4
and
6
, stating that if
\Omega
splits into
\Omega^{\prime}
and a variable
u
is mapped to
\mathcal{U}
in
\Omega^{\prime}
, then
u
is mapped to a subtype of
\mathcal{U}
in
\Omega
. An additional consequence of Lemma
5
is that
\Omega
context splitting cannot introduce new domain elements: if
\Omega
splits into
\Omega^{\prime}
, then only variables and generators within
\Omega
may appear within
\Omega^{\prime}
.

Lemma 0.

If
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

then
\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

•

US:Prod
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\square}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\blacksquare}
.
Apply
UT:ProdLeft
and
UT:ProdRight
.

•

US:SplitBoth
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Apply
UT:Prod
twice.

•

US:SplitLeft
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\square}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By
UT:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime a}
.
By
UT:ProdRight
,

\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime a}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
UT:Transitive
.

•

US:SplitRight
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

US:Corecursive
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By
UT:Rec1
,

\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
.
Apply
UT:Transitive
twice.

•

US:Subtype
.
Then
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
and
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
UT:Transitive
.

•

US:Commutative
.
By induction.

Lemma 0.

If
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
then:

(1)

If
u:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{1}

then
u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.

(2)

If
u:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{2}

then
u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.

(3)

If
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{1}

then
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.

(4)

If
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{2}

then
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
.

•

OM:Commutative
.
By induction.

•

OM:Var
.
Then
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime},u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
.

(1)

u:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{1}
.
There are two cases:

(a)

u=u^{\prime}
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
u:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega
.

(b)

u\neq u^{\prime}
.
Then
u:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
.
By induction,
u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Therefore,
u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega
.

(2)

u:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{2}
.

u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Therefore,
u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega
.

(3)

\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{1}
.
Then
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
.

\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Therefore,
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega
.

(4)

\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\in\Omega_{2}
.
By induction,
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Therefore,
\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega
.

•

OM:Gen
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

.
Then
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime},u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
and
\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}
.

.

Lemma 0.

If
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{0}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

then
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

U:OmegaVar
.
Then
U=u

and
\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}
.
By Lemma
5
,

u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\Omega

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
By
U:OmegaVar
,

\Omega;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

•

U:OmegaSeed
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:PsiVar
.
Apply
U:PsiVar
.

•

U:PsiSeed
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Omega_{1}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega_{1}^{\prime};\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime};\Psi\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}
.
By induction,

\Omega^{\prime};\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
Apply
U:Pair
.

•

All other cases are by induction and self-application.

\frac{\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}\quad\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{1}:*\quad\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash G_{2}:*}{\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}:*}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(DW:Par)}\frac{e_{1}\Downarrow v_{1}\mid g_{1}\quad e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}\mid g_{2}}{\mathsf{par}(e_{1},e_{2})\Downarrow(v_{1},v_{2})\mid g_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}g_{2}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(C:Par)}\frac{\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}\quad\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e_{1}:\tau_{1}\mid G_{1}\quad\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{2}\mid G_{2}}{\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{par}(e_{1},e_{2}):\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}\mid G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(S:Par)}

\begin{array}[]{l r l l}\\
\mathit{Graph~Types}&G&\mathop{::=}&\dots\mid G\mathbin{\otimes}G\\
\mathit{Expressions}&e&\mathop{::=}&\dots\mid\mathsf{par}(e,e)\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{r l l l}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2})&\triangleq&\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1})\mathbin{\otimes}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2})\\
\mathit{Exp}(G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2})&\triangleq&\{g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}g_{2}^{\prime}\mid g_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(G_{1}),g_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(G_{2})&\\
(V_{1},E_{1},s_{1},t_{1})\mathbin{\otimes}(V_{2},E_{2},s_{2},t_{2})&\triangleq&(V_{1}\cup V_{2}\cup\{u_{1},u_{2}\},E_{1}\cup E_{2}\cup&u_{1},u_{2}~\mathsf{fresh},\\
&&~~\{(u_{1},s_{1}),(u_{1},s_{2}),(t_{1},u_{2}),(t_{2},u_{2})\},u_{1},u_{2})&V_{1}\cap V_{2}=\emptyset\\
\end{array}

.
Adding fork-join parallelism to
\lambda^{G\mu}
.

Figures
32
,
33
, and
34
give the full rules for graph type formation,
VS equivalence, and type constructor equivalence respectively.
Figure
35
adds the syntax and rules for a fork-join parallel expression and graph type.
Fork-join parallellism in
\lambda^{G\mu}
functions similar to how it does in
Muller 2022
.
The expression
\mathsf{par}(e_{1},e_{2})
executes
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
in parallel, and this expression
is given the graph type
G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}
where
e_{1}
has graph type
G_{1}

and
e_{2}
has graph type
G_{2}
.

Lemma
9
shows that well-formed VSs, expressions, type
constructors, and graph types maintain their types and kinds when substituting
any free variables appropriately. In addition, substituting variables in
equivalent VSs and type constructors maintains their equivalence. When
substituting VS variables found in the affine context
\Omega
, we require that
the term undergoing the substitution and the VS being substituted
are well-typed under two disjoint contexts
\Omega_{1}
and

\Omega_{2}
, and the resulting term is well-typed under their conjoinment (any

\Omega
where
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
). When substituting a
VS variable
u
with a VS
U
in expressions and graph types, there are
two distinct cases:
u
is in both
\Omega
and
\Psi
and
U
is
well-typed under a disjoint
\Omega^{\prime}
and separately well-typed under
\Psi
,
or
u
is only in
\Psi
and
U
is well-typed under
\Psi
(when
assigning types to expressions and graph kinds to graph types, it never occurs
that a variable is only in
\Omega
). We make these cases separate so that
substituting variables in
\Psi
does not cause vertices only found within

\Psi
to appear within future spawns, but variables in
\Psi
that do not
appear within future spawns can be more freely substituted for. Since we always
require that VSs are either typed under an empty
\Omega
or an empty
\Psi

in the rules for other judgements,
we have two cases for substituting VS variables that reflect that: either the
variable is in
\Omega
and
\Psi
is empty, or vice-versa.

This substitution lemma requires Lemmas
7
and
8
.
Lemma
7
states that if a VS

U
is given a type
\mathcal{U}
under some
\Omega
, and
\mathcal{U}

splits into
\mathcal{U}_{1}
and
\mathcal{U}_{2}
, then
\Omega
must split into some

\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
where
U
has type
\mathcal{U}_{1}
under
\Omega_{1}
and
has type
\mathcal{U}_{2}
under
\Omega_{2}
.
Lemma
8
states that the affine restriction can be removed
from a VS typing judgement and that judgement still holds.

Lemma 0.

If
\Omega;\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

then there exists an
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}

such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\Omega;\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.
We prove some representative cases.

•

U:OmegaVar
.
Then
U=u

and
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}
.
Since
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\cdot
,
by
OM:TypeSplit
,

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus u:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
U:Omega
twice.

•

U:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}\boxplus\Omega_{4}

and
\Omega_{3};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{3}

and
\Omega_{4};\cdot\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{4}
.
By Lemma
1
, there are seven cases:

(1)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\Omega_{3};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
By
U:OnlyRightPair
,

\Omega_{4};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
twice.

(2)

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(3)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,
there exists
\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
and
\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Omega_{3}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Omega_{3}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{4}
.
By
U:Pair
,

\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.
By
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

(4)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(5)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(6)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(7)

There exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{4}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,
there exists
\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
and
\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Omega_{3}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Omega_{3}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}

and there exists
\Omega_{4}^{\prime}
and
\Omega_{4}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Omega_{4}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{4}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{4}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Omega_{4}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{4}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{4}^{\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{4}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
U:Pair
,

\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\blacksquare}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.
By
U:Pair
,

\Omega^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

•

U:OnlyLeftPair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}

and
\Omega;\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{3}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{4}
.
By Lemma
1
,
there exists
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{3}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,
there exists
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
such that

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
and

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.
By
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{4}^{\square}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

•

U:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime}

and
\Omega;\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a}
.
By
US:SplitLeft
,

\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}
.
By induction,
there exists
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
such that

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
and

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}^{a}
and

\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}^{\square}
.
Apply
U:Fst
twice.

•

U:Subtype
.
Then
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}

and
\Omega;\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
2
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply induction.

∎

The proof for Lemma
8
is straightforward, so it is ommited.

Lemma 0.

If
\Omega;\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

then
\cdot;\Omega\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

Lemma 0.

(1)

If
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}\vdash e:\tau\mid G

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi;\cdot\vdash v:\tau^{\prime}\mid\bullet

then
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e[v/x]:\tau\mid G
.

(2)

If
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon,\alpha::\kappa^{\prime}\vdash c::\kappa

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime}::\kappa^{\prime}

then
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c[c^{\prime}/\alpha]::\kappa
.

(3)

If
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G}^{\prime};\Omega;\Psi\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

and
\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}:\kappa_{G}^{\prime}

then
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G[G^{\prime}/\gamma]:\kappa_{G}
.

(4)

If
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c::\kappa

and
\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

then
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c[G/\gamma]::\kappa
.

(5)

If
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}\equiv c_{2}::\kappa

and
\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

then
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}[G/\gamma]\equiv c_{2}[G/\gamma]::\kappa
.

(6)

If
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\mid G

and
\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}:\kappa_{G}

then
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\vdash e:\tau[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\mid G[G^{\prime}/\gamma]
.

(7)

If
\cdot;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

then
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}
.

(8)

If
\Omega,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}

then
\Omega^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}
.

(9)

If
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
u\notin\Omega

then
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G[U/u]:\kappa_{G}
.

(10)

If
\Delta;\Omega,u:\mathcal{U};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

and
\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}

then
\Delta;\Omega^{\prime\prime};\Psi\vdash G[U/u]:\kappa_{G}
.

(11)

If
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash c::\kappa

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

then
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c[U/u]::\kappa
.

(12)

If
\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U_{1}\equiv U_{2}:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

then
\Psi\vdash U_{1}[U/u]\equiv U_{2}[U/u]:\mathcal{U}
.

(13)

If
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}\equiv c_{2}::\kappa

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

then
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}[U/u]\equiv c_{2}[U/u]::\kappa
.

(14)

If
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\mid G

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
u\notin\Omega

then
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e[U/u]:\tau[U/u]\mid G[U/u]
.

(15)

If
\Delta;\Omega,u:\mathcal{U};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\mid G

and
\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}

then
\Delta;\Omega^{\prime\prime};\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e[U/u]:\tau[U/u]\mid G[U/u]
.

Proof.

We show the interesting cases for all parts except (
1
),
which is standard.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of

\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}\vdash e:\tau\mid G
.

(2)

By induction on the derivation of

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon,\alpha::\kappa^{\prime}\vdash c::\kappa
.

•

K:Rec
.
Then
c=\mu(\alpha^{\prime};u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime};U)

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty},\alpha::\kappa^{\prime}\vdash c^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Since
\alpha^{\prime}
is bound inside
c
,
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash c^{\prime\prime}[c^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
K:Rec
.

(3)

By induction on the derivation
of
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G}^{\prime};\Omega;\Psi\vdash G:\kappa_{G}
.

•

DW:RecPi
.
Then
G=\mu\gamma^{\prime}.\Pi[u_{f};u_{t}].G^{\prime\prime}

and
\kappa_{G}=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

and
\Delta,\gamma^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*,\gamma:\kappa_{G}^{\prime};u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G:*
.
By induction,

\Delta,\gamma^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G^{\prime\prime}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]:*
.
Apply
DW:RecPi
.

(4)

By induction on the derivation
of
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c::\kappa
.

•

K:Fun
.
Then
c=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].c_{1}\xrightarrow{G^{\prime}[u_{f};u_{t}]}c_{2}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Upsilon\vdash c_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\cdot;\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}[G/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Upsilon\vdash c_{2}[G/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By part
3
,

\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}[G/\gamma]:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
Apply
K:Fun
.

(5)

By induction on the derivation of

\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}\equiv c_{2}::\kappa
.

•

CE:Reflexive
.
Then
c_{1}=c_{2}
,
and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}::\kappa
.
By part
4
,

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}[G/\gamma]::\kappa
.
Apply
CE:Reflexive
.

•

CE:Prod
.
Then
c_{1}=c_{3}\times c_{4}

and
c_{2}=c_{3}^{\prime}\times c_{4}^{\prime}

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{3}\equiv c_{3}^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{4}\equiv c_{4}^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{3}[G/\gamma]\equiv c_{3}^{\prime}[G/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{4}[G/\gamma]\equiv c_{4}^{\prime}[G/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
CE:Prod
.

(6)

By induction on the derivation
of
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\mid G
.

•

S:Fun
.
Then
\tau=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{(\mu\gamma^{\prime}.\Pi[u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}].G^{\prime\prime})[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}

and
e=\mathsf{fun}[u_{f};u_{t}]~f~x=e^{\prime}

and
G=\bullet

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G},\gamma^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Gamma,f:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma^{\prime}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2},x:\tau_{1}\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau_{2}\mid G^{\prime\prime}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta,\gamma^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Gamma[G^{\prime}/\gamma],f:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\xrightarrow{\gamma^{\prime}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}[G^{\prime}/\gamma],x:\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau_{2}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\mid G^{\prime\prime}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]
.
By part
4
,

\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
S:Fun
.

•

S:Type-Eq
.
Then
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau_{1}\mid G

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}\equiv\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\vdash e:\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\mid G[G^{\prime}/\gamma]
.
By part
5
,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\equiv\tau[G^{\prime}/\gamma]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
S:Type-Eq
.

•

S:App
.
Then
e={e_{1}[U_{f};U_{t}]}~e_{2}

and
\tau=\tau_{2}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}\boxplus\Omega_{3}

and
G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{3}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Omega_{1};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e_{1}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G_{3}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}\mid G_{1}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\kappa_{G};\Omega_{2};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{1}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}

and
\Omega_{3};\cdot\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi;\Gamma[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\vdash e_{1}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\xrightarrow{G_{3}[G^{\prime}/\gamma][u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\mid G_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]

and
\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi;\Gamma[G^{\prime}/\gamma]\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{1}[G^{\prime}/\gamma][U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}[G^{\prime}/\gamma]
.

Apply
S:App
.

(7)

By induction on the derivation of

\cdot;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

U:PsiVar
.
Then
U=u^{\prime}

and
\Psi=\Psi^{\prime},u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Since
u\neq u^{\prime}
,

u^{\prime}[U^{\prime}/u]=u^{\prime}
.
Apply
U:PsiVar
.

•

U:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\cdot\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By inversion on
OM:Empty
,

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{2}=\cdot
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{2}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
U:Pair
.

•

U:OnlyLeftPair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By weakening and induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime},\Psi\vdash U_{2}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
U:OnlyLeftPair
.

•

U:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
U:Fst
.

(8)

By induction on the derivation of

\Omega,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

U:OmegaVar
.
Then
U=u^{\prime}

and
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime},u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Since
u\neq u^{\prime}
,

U[U^{\prime}/u]=u^{\prime}
.
Apply Lemma
6
.

•

U:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Omega,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By Lemma
1
there are four cases:

(a)

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
u\notin\Omega_{2}
.
Therefore,

U_{2}
does not contain
u
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
Apply
U:Pair
.

(b)

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime}

and
u\notin\Omega_{1}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(c)

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
and

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
7
,
there exists
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{2}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
U:Pair
.

(d)

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
and

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:OnlyLeftPair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}

and
\Omega,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\Omega^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{1}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
By weakening,

\cdot;\Psi^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By Lemma
8
,

\cdot;\Omega^{\prime}\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By weakening and part
7
,

\cdot;\Psi^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
U:OnlyLeftPair
.

•

U:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega,u:\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By induction,

\Omega^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime\prime}[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
U:Fst
.

(9)

By induction on the derivation of

DW:App

Then
G=G^{\prime}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
\kappa_{G}=*

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}\vdash G^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

.

,

u\notin\Omega_{1}

and
u\notin\Omega_{2}
.

U_{f}
does not contain
u
.

\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}[U/u]:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

DW:App

Then

,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime};\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e_{2}[U/u]:\tau_{1}[U/u][U_{f}[U/u]/u_{f}][U_{t}[U/u]/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}[U/u]
.
By part
8
,

\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{f}[U/u]:\mathcal{U}_{f}
.
Apply
S:App
.

(iv)

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}
and

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{3}
and

\Omega_{3}=\Omega_{3}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{2}
and

\Omega_{0}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

(c)

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{0}
and

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\Omega_{0}=\Omega_{0}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{0}
and

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
7
,
there exists
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{0}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{0}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e_{1}[U/u]:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}[U/u]\xrightarrow{G_{3}[U/u][u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}[U/u]\mid G_{1}[U/u]
.
By Lemma
1
there are four cases:

(i)

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{0}
and
\Omega_{0}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}

and
u\notin\Omega_{3}
.
Therefore,

U_{f}
does not contain
u
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime};\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e_{2}[U/u]:\tau_{1}[U/u][U_{f}[U/u]/u_{f}][U_{t}[U/u]/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}[U/u]

Apply
S:App
.

(ii)

\Omega_{3}=\Omega_{3}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{0}
and
\Omega_{0}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime}

and
u\notin\Omega_{2}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.
By part
14
,

\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e_{2}[U/u]:\tau_{1}[U/u][U_{f}[U/u]/u_{f}][U_{t}[U/u]/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}[U/u]
.
By part
8
,

\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{f}[U/u]:\mathcal{U}_{f}
.
Apply
S:App
.

(iii)

\mathcal{U}_{0}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}
and

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{2}
and

\Omega_{3}=\Omega_{3}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{3}
and

\Omega_{0}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
7
,
there exists
\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Omega_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
and

\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{2}
and

\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U:\mathcal{U}_{3}
.
By Lemma
3
,
there exists
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{3}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime};\Psi;\Gamma[U/u]\vdash e_{2}[U/u]:\tau_{1}[U/u][U_{f}[U/u]/u_{f}][U_{t}[U/u]/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}[U/u]
.
By part
8
,

\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash U_{f}[U/u]:\mathcal{U}_{f}
.
Apply
S:App
.

(iv)

\mathcal{U}_{0}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{2}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{3}
and

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{3}
and

\Omega_{3}=\Omega_{3}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{2}
and

\Omega_{0}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.
