\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{0}
and

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{0}
and

\Omega_{0}=\Omega_{0}^{\prime},u:\mathcal{U}_{1}
and

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

∎

Lemmas
10
shows that VS type assignments in VS equivalence judgements are consistent with the VS typing judgements:
if
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
are equivalent VSs with type
\mathcal{U}
, then
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
can both be given type
\mathcal{U}
with a VS typing judgement.
Lemma
11
shows the same property for type constructor equivalence with regards to kinding.

Lemma 0.

If
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}

then
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.

•

UE:Reflexive
.
Then
U=U^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

UE:Commutative
.
Then
\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U:\mathcal{U}
.
Apply induction.

•

UE:Transitive
.
Then
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}

and
\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime}\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Apply induction.

•

UE:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
U^{\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{1}\equiv U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{2}\equiv U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
OM:Empty
and
U:Pair
twice.

•

UE:OnlyLeftPair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
U^{\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{1}\equiv U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}\equiv U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
U:OnlyLeftPair
twice.

•

UE:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime}

and
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime}\equiv U^{\prime\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
U:Fst
twice.

•

UE:FstPair
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime}

and
\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime}\equiv(U^{\prime},U_{2}):\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash(U^{\prime},U_{2}):\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
U:Fst
.

By inversion on the VS typing rules, there are two cases:

(1)

By inversion on
U:Subtype
and
U:Pair
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\cdot\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
OM:Empty
,

\Omega_{1}=\cdot
.
By inversion on the VS subtyping rules,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

(2)

By inversion on
U:Subtype
and
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the VS subtyping rules,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

•

UE:Subtype
.
Then
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
By induction,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
twice.

•

All other cases are by symmetry with another case of the proof.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c\equiv c^{\prime}::\kappa

then
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c::\kappa

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime}::\kappa
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c\equiv c^{\prime}::\kappa
.

•

CE:Reflexive
.
Then
c=c^{\prime}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c::\kappa
.

•

CE:Commutative
.
By induction.

•

CE:Transitive
.
By induction.

•

CE:Prod
.
Then
c=c_{1}\times c_{2}

and
c^{\prime}=c_{1}^{\prime}\times c_{2}^{\prime}

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}\equiv c_{1}^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{2}\equiv c_{2}^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{1}^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c_{2}^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
K:Prod
twice.

•

CE:Sum
.
Similar to the previous case.

•

CE:Fut
.
Then
c=c^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U]

and
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime\prime\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U^{\prime}]

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}\equiv c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By Lemma
10
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge
.
Apply
K:Fut
twice.

•

CE:Lambda
.
Then
c=\lambda u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime}

and
c^{\prime}=\lambda u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
\kappa=\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}\equiv c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
K:Lambda
twice.

•

CE:App
.
Then
c=c^{\prime\prime}~U

and
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime\prime\prime}~U^{\prime}

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}\equiv c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
and
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
By Lemma
10
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
K:App
twice.

•

CE:BetaEq
.
Then
c=(\lambda u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime})~U

and
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime\prime}[U/u]

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash c^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.
By
K:Lambda
,

\Delta;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash\lambda u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime}::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
K:App
.
Apply Lemma
9
.

•

CE:Rec
.
Then
c=\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime};U)

and
c^{\prime}=\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.c^{\prime\prime\prime};U^{\prime})

and
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash c^{\prime\prime}\equiv c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\kappa=\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash c^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash c^{\prime\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By Lemma
10
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
K:Rec
twice.

∎

Lemma
12
establishes that the type system for
\lambda^{G\mu}
only gives well-formed types and graph types to expressions. However, this is only true for expressions typed under well-formed contexts. To formalize what it means for a context to be well-formed, we extend the definition of type formation to
\Gamma
contexts in the natural way:
\Gamma
is well-formed if all of the types in it are. The contexts
\Delta
,
\Omega
, and
\Psi
are always well-formed. The judgment
\Delta;\Psi\vdash\Gamma
denotes that
\Gamma
is well-formed under
\Delta
and
\Psi
. Using the rules for this judgement, we can now prove that if an expression has type
\tau
and
graph type
G
under well-formed contexts, then
\tau
has kind
\mathsf{Ty}
and
G
has graph kind
*
.

\frac{}{\Delta;\Psi\vdash\cdot}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(CN:Empty)}\frac{\Delta;\Psi\vdash\Gamma\quad\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}}{\Delta;\Psi\vdash\Gamma,x:\tau}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(CN:Elem)}

Lemma 0.

If
\Delta;\Psi\vdash\Gamma

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\mid G

then
\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G:*
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\mid G
.
We prove some representative cases.

•

S:Var
.
Then
e=x

and
G=\bullet

and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},x:\tau
.
By inversion on
CN:Elem
,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
DW:Empty
.

•

S:Fun
.
Then
\tau=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{(\mu\gamma.\Pi[u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}].G^{\prime})[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}

and
G=\bullet

and
e=\mathsf{fun}[u_{f};u_{t}]~f~x=e^{\prime}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\Gamma,f:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2},x:\tau_{1}\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau_{2}\mid G^{\prime}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
DW:Empty
,

\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash\bullet:*
.
By
DW:Var
,

\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;\cdot;\Psi\vdash\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
By
K:Fun
,

\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By weakening and two applications of
CN:Elem
,

\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash\Gamma,f:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2},x:\tau_{1}
.
By induction,

\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G^{\prime}:*
.
By
DW:RecPi
,

\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash\mu\gamma.\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
Apply
K:Fun
.

•

S:App
.
Then
e={e_{1}[U_{f};U_{t}]}~e_{2}

and
\tau=\tau_{2}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}\boxplus\Omega_{3}

and
G=G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{3}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e_{1}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G_{3}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}\mid G_{1}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{1}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}

and
\Omega_{3};\cdot\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G_{3}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By inversion on
K:Fun
,

\Delta;\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G_{3}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
By two applications of Lemma
9
,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
DW:App
,

\Delta;\Omega_{3};\Psi\vdash G_{3}[U_{f};U_{t}]:*
.
Apply
DW:Seq
twice.

•

S:Roll
.
Then
e=\mathsf{roll}~e^{\prime}

and
\tau=\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};U)

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau^{\prime}[U/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]\mid G

and
\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction.

•

S:Unroll
.
Then
e=\mathsf{unroll}~e^{\prime}

and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}[U/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}:\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};U)\mid G
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};U)::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G:*
.
By inversion on
K:Rec
,

\Delta;\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\alpha::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.
By
U:PsiVar
,

\cdot;\Psi,u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}\vdash u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
By weakening and
K:Rec
,

\Delta;\Psi,u^{\prime}:\mathcal{U};\cdot\vdash\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
K:Lambda
,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
By two applications of Lemma
9
.

•

S:Future
.
Then
e=\mathsf{future}[U]~e^{\prime}

and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U]

and
G=G^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}\mid G^{\prime}

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}:*
.
Apply
K:Fut
.
Apply
DW:Spawn
.

•

S:Touch
.
Then
e=\mathsf{touch}~e^{\prime}

and
G=G^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau~\mathsf{future}[U]\mid G^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau~\mathsf{future}[U]::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}:*
.
By inversion on
K:Fut
,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By
DW:Touch
,

\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}:*
.
Apply
DW:Seq
.

•

S:New
.
Then
e=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.e^{\prime}

and
G=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime}

and
\Delta;\Omega,u:\mathcal{U};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau\mid G^{\prime}

and
\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega,u:\mathcal{U};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U}\vdash G^{\prime}:*
.
Apply
DW:New
.

•

S:Type-Eq
.
Then
\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}\equiv\tau::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash e:\tau_{1}\mid G
.
By Lemma
11
,

\Delta;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction.

Appendix B
From Section
4

\frac{U\Downarrow(U_{1},U_{2})}{\mathsf{snd}~U\Downarrow U_{2}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UV:SndPair)}\frac{U\Downarrow U^{\prime}\quad U^{\prime}\neq(U_{1},U_{2})}{\mathsf{snd}~U\Downarrow\mathsf{snd}~U^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UV:SndNotPair)}

Figure 36
.
Rules for evaluating
\mathsf{snd}~U
.

Figure
36
gives the rules for evaluating
\mathsf{snd}
projections of VS
which are symmetric to those for
\mathsf{fst}
projections in Figure
16
.
Together, Figures
16
and
36
form the complete ruleset for VS evaluation.

We now prove some properties involving the interactions between VS typing, VS evaluation, and VS equivalence.
Lemma
1
proves a combined progress and preservation property for VS evaluation.
Lemma
2
shows that evaluation implies equivalence:
a well-formed VS
U
is equivalent to any VS that
U
evaluates to.
Lemma
3
shows that equivalent VSs evaluate to the same VSs:
if
U
evaluates to
U^{\prime}
, then any VSs equivalent to
U
also evaluate to
U^{\prime}
.

Lemma 0.

If
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

then there exists a
U^{\prime}
such that
U\Downarrow U^{\prime}

and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\Omega;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

U:OmegaVar
,
U:PsiVar
.
Then
U=u
. By
UV:Var
,
U\Downarrow U
. True by premise.

•

U:OmegaGen
,
U:PsiGen
.
Then
U=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
. By
UV:Path
,
U\Downarrow U
. True by premise.

•

U:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

U_{1}\Downarrow U^{\prime}_{1}

and
U_{2}\Downarrow U^{\prime}_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By
UV:Pair
,

U\Downarrow(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})
.
Apply
U:Pair
.

•

U:OnlyLeftPair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}

and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

U_{1}\Downarrow U^{\prime}_{1}

and
U_{2}\Downarrow U^{\prime}_{2}

and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By
UV:Pair
,

U\Downarrow(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})
.
Apply
U:OnlyLeftPair
.

•

U:OnlyRightPair
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}

and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U_{0}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By induction,

U_{0}\Downarrow U_{0}^{\prime}
and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U_{0}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
There are two cases:

(1)

U_{0}^{\prime}\neq(U_{1},U_{2})
.
By
UV:FstNotPair
,

U\Downarrow\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}^{\prime}
.
Apply
U:Fst
.

(2)

U_{0}^{\prime}=(U_{1},U_{2})
.
By
UV:FstPair
,

U\Downarrow U_{1}
.
By inversion on the VS typing rules, there are two cases:

(a)

By inversion on
U:Subtype
and
U:Pair
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\blacksquare}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
6
,

\Omega;\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the VS subtyping rules,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

(b)

By inversion on
U:Subtype
and
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime\square}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the VS subtyping rules,

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

•

U:Snd
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:Subtype
.
Then
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
By induction,

U\Downarrow U^{\prime}

and
\Omega;\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
Apply
U:Subtype
.

Lemma 0.

If
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}

and
U\Downarrow U^{\prime}

then
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

U:OmegaVar
,
U:PsiVar
.
Then
U=u
. By inversion on
UV:Var
,
U=U^{\prime}
. Apply
UE:Reflexive
.

•

U:OmegaGen
,
U:PsiGen
.
Then
U=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
. By inversion on
UV:Path
,
U=U^{\prime}
. Apply
UE:Reflexive
.

•

U:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\cdot\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By inversion on
OM:Empty
,

\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{2}=\cdot
.
By inversion on
UV:Pair
,

U^{\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})

and
U_{1}\Downarrow U_{1}^{\prime}

and
U_{2}\Downarrow U_{2}^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\Psi\vdash U_{1}\equiv U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{2}\equiv U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
UE:Pair
.

•

U:OnlyLeftPair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{1}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By inversion on
UV:Pair
,

U^{\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})

and
U_{1}\Downarrow U_{1}^{\prime}

and
U_{2}\Downarrow U_{2}^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\Psi\vdash U_{1}\equiv U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Psi^{\prime}\vdash U_{2}\equiv U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
UE:OnlyLeftPair
.

•

U:OnlyRightPair
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{0}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By inversion on the VS evaluation rules, there are two cases:

(1)

By inversion on
UV:FstNotPair
,

U^{\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}^{\prime}

U_{0}\Downarrow U_{0}^{\prime}
.
By induction,

\Psi\vdash U_{0}\equiv U_{0}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
UE:Fst
.

(2)

By inversion on
UV:FstPair
,

U_{0}\Downarrow(U^{\prime},U_{2})
.
By induction,

\Psi\vdash U_{0}\equiv(U^{\prime},U_{2}):\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
UE:FstPair
.

•

U:Snd
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

U:Subtype
.
Then
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
By induction,

\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
Apply
UE:Subtype
.

Lemma 0.

If
U\Downarrow U^{\prime\prime}

and
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}

then
U^{\prime}\Downarrow U^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
or
\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U:\mathcal{U}
.

•

UE:Reflexive
.
Then
U=U^{\prime}
.
True by premise of the lemma.

•

UE:Commutative
.
Apply induction.

•

UE:Transitive
.

–

\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Then
\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime\prime\prime}:\mathcal{U}

and
\Psi\vdash U^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
By induction,

U^{\prime\prime\prime}\Downarrow U^{\prime\prime}
.
Apply induction.

–

\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U:\mathcal{U}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

UE:Pair
.

–

\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
U^{\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})

and
\Psi\vdash U_{1}\equiv U_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{2}\equiv U_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By inversion on
UV:Pair
,

U^{\prime\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime\prime},U_{2}^{\prime\prime})

and
U_{1}\Downarrow U_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
U_{2}\Downarrow U_{2}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

U_{1}^{\prime}\Downarrow U_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
U_{2}^{\prime}\Downarrow U_{2}^{\prime\prime}
.
Apply
UV:Pair
.

–

\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U:\mathcal{U}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

UE:Fst
.

–

\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}

and
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}^{\prime}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{0}\equiv U_{0}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By inversion on the VS evaluation rules, there are two cases:

*

By inversion on
UV:FstPair
,

U_{0}\Downarrow(U^{\prime\prime},U_{2})
.
By induction,

U_{0}^{\prime}\Downarrow(U^{\prime\prime},U_{2})
.
Apply
UV:FstPair
.

*

By inversion on
UV:FstNotPair
,

U^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}^{\prime\prime}

and
U_{0}\Downarrow U_{0}^{\prime\prime}

and
U_{0}^{\prime\prime}\neq(U_{1},U_{2})
.
By induction,

U_{0}^{\prime}\Downarrow U_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.
Apply
UV:FstNotPair
.

–

\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U:\mathcal{U}
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

UE:FstPair
.

–

\Psi\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{0}\equiv(U^{\prime},U_{2}):\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By inversion on the VS evaluation rules, there are two cases:

*

By inversion on
UV:FstPair
,

U_{0}\Downarrow(U^{\prime\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})
.
By induction,

(U^{\prime},U_{2})\Downarrow(U^{\prime\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})
.
By inversion on
UV:Pair
,

U^{\prime}\Downarrow U^{\prime\prime}
.

*

By inversion on
UV:FstNotPair
,

U^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}^{\prime\prime}

and
U_{0}\Downarrow U_{0}^{\prime\prime}

and
U_{0}^{\prime\prime}\neq(U_{1},U_{2})
.
By induction,

(U^{\prime},U_{2})\Downarrow U_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.
By inversion on
UV:Pair
,

U_{0}^{\prime\prime}=(U_{1},U_{2})
,
which contradicts
U_{0}^{\prime\prime}\neq(U_{1},U_{2})
,
so this case does not apply.

–

\Psi\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U:\mathcal{U}
.
Then
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U_{0}^{\prime}

and
\Psi\vdash U_{0}^{\prime}\equiv(U,U_{2}):\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By Lemma
10
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash(U,U_{2}):\mathcal{U}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
By inversion on
U:Subtype
and either
U:Pair
or
U:OnlyLeftPair
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{2}:\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
1
,
there exists a
U_{2}^{\prime}
such that
U_{2}\Downarrow U_{2}^{\prime}
.
By
UV:Pair
,

(U,U_{2})\Downarrow(U^{\prime\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})
.
By induction,

U_{0}^{\prime}\Downarrow(U^{\prime\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})
.
Apply
UV:FstPair
.

•

UE:Subtype
.
Apply induction.

•

All other cases are by symmetry with another case of the proof.

∎

We now show that the three-step normalization
process can be performed on any well-formed graph type and results in a set
of well-formed graphs.
The first step is to show that graph type unrolling and conversion to
NBNF both preserve well-formedness of graphs.
This in particular ensures that affine restrictions on vertices are maintained,
which is necessary to ensure that when a graph is unrolled and converted to
NBNF, it can still be expanded into a well-formed graph (recall that duplicate
vertices will make a graph ill-formed).
Lemma
4
shows that graph type unrolling preserves
well-formedness.
Lemma
5
shows that for a well-formed graph type,
there exists an NBNF, and the NBNF of the graph type is still well-formed.
Finally, we show (Lemma
12
)
that if a well-formed graph type in NBNF is expanded, all of
the graphs in the resulting set are well-formed graphs (in particular, have no
duplicate vertices).

Lemma 0.

If
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

then
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G^{\prime}:\kappa_{G}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
.

•

UR:Seq1
.
Then
G=G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}

and
G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:Seq
,

\kappa_{G}=*

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{1}^{\prime}:*
.
Apply rule
DW:Seq
.

•

UR:Seq2
. Symmetric to above.

•

UR:Par1
. Then
G=G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}

and
G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:Par
,

\kappa_{G}=*

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\Delta;\Omega_{2};\Psi\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{1}^{\prime}:*
.
Apply rule
DW:Par
.

•

UR:Par2
. Symmetric to above.

•

UR:Or1
. Then
G=G_{1}\lor G_{2}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime}\lor G_{2}

and
G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:Or
,

\kappa_{G}=*

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By induction,

\Delta;\Omega;\Psi\vdash G_{1}^{\prime}:*
.
Apply rule
DW:Or
.

•

UR:Or2
. Symmetric to above.

•

UR:Future
.
Then
G=G_{0}\!\sswarrow_{U}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{0}^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}

and
G_{0}\hookrightarrow G_{0}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:Spawn
,

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{0}:*

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By induction,
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{0}^{\prime}:*
.
Apply
DW:Spawn
.

•

UR:Rec
.
Then
G=\mu\gamma.G_{0}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{0}[\mu\gamma.G_{0}/\gamma]
.
By inversion on
DW:RecPi
,

\kappa_{G}=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

and
G_{0}=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{0}^{\prime}

and
\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G_{0}^{\prime}:*
.
By
DW:RecPi
,

\Delta;\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mu\gamma.G_{0}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
By
DW:Pi
,

\Delta,\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;\cdot;\Psi\vdash G_{0}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
By multiple applications of
OM:Var
and
OM:Gen
,

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega\boxplus\cdot
.
Apply Lemma
9
and Lemma
6
.

•

UR:Pi
.
Then
G=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{0}

and
G^{\prime}=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{0}^{\prime}

and
G_{0}\hookrightarrow G_{0}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:Pi
,

\Delta;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G_{0}:*
.
By induction,

\Delta;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G_{0}^{\prime}:*
.
Apply
DW:Pi
.

•

UR:App
.
Then
G=G_{0}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
G^{\prime}=G_{0}^{\prime}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
G_{0}\hookrightarrow G_{0}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:App
,

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{0}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}
.
By induction,
\Delta;\Omega_{1};\Psi\vdash G_{0}^{\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
Apply
DW:App
.

•

UR:New
.
Then
G=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G_{0}

and
G^{\prime}=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G_{0}^{\prime}

and
G_{0}\hookrightarrow G_{0}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
DW:New
,

\Delta;\Omega,u\!:\!\mathcal{U};\Psi,u\!:\!\mathcal{U}\vdash G_{0}:*
.
By induction,
\Delta;\Omega,u\!:\!\mathcal{U};\Psi,u\!:\!\mathcal{U}\vdash G_{0}^{\prime}:*
.
Apply
DW:New
.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G:\kappa_{G}

then there exist
G^{\prime}
,
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{0}
, and
\Psi^{\circ}_{0}

such that
G^{\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G)

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{0}
and
\Psi^{\circ}_{0}
consist of only fresh generators,
and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{0};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{0}\vdash G^{\prime}:\kappa_{G}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation
of
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G:\kappa_{G}
.

•

DW:Empty
.
Then
G=\bullet
.
We have
\mathsf{NBNF}(G)=G
.

•

DW:Var
. This case cannot occur since
\Delta
is empty.

•

DW:Seq
.
Then
G=G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By induction,
there exist
G_{1}^{\prime}
,
G_{2}^{\prime}
,
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}
,
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{4}
,
\Psi^{\circ}_{3}
, and
\Psi^{\circ}_{4}

such that
G_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1})

and
G_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2})
,
and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}
,
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{4}
,
\Psi^{\circ}_{3}
, and
\Psi^{\circ}_{4}
consist of only fresh generators,
and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{3}\vdash G_{1}^{\prime}:*

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{4};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{4}\vdash G_{2}^{\prime}:*
.
Let
G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}^{\prime}

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{0}={\Omega^{\circ}}_{3},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{4}

and
\Psi^{\circ}_{0}=\Psi^{\circ}_{3},\Psi^{\circ}_{4}
.
We have
G^{\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G)
.
By multiple applications of
OM:Gen
and
OM:Commutative
,

{\Omega^{\circ}},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{0}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{4}
.
Apply weakening and
DW:Seq
.

•

DW:Par
,
DW:OR
. Similar to above.

•

DW:RecPi
.
Then
G=\mu\gamma.\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G^{\prime}
.
We have
\mathsf{NBNF}(G)=G
.

•

DW:Spawn
.
Then
G=G^{\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

and
\Delta;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G^{\prime\prime}:*

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By induction, there exists
G^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}
, and
\Psi^{\circ}_{3}
such
that
G^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime})
,
and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}
and
\Psi^{\circ}_{3}
consist of only fresh generators,
and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{3}\vdash G^{\prime\prime\prime}:*
.
By Lemma
1
, there exists
U^{\prime}
such that

U\Downarrow U^{\prime}
and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge
.
We have
G^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G)
.
By multiple applications of
OM:Gen
,

{\Omega^{\circ}},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}
.
Apply
DW:Spawn
.

•

DW:Touch
.
Then
\cdot;\Psi^{\circ}\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By Lemma
1
, there exists
U^{\prime}
such that

U\Downarrow U^{\prime}
and
\cdot;\Psi^{\circ}\vdash U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge
.
We have
\mathsf{NBNF}(\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow})=\mathop{{}^{U^{\prime}}\!\ssearrow}
.
Apply
DW:Touch
.

•

DW:New
.
Then
G=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime\prime}

and
\kappa_{G}=*

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}},u:\mathcal{U};\Psi^{\circ},u:\mathcal{U}\vdash G^{\prime\prime}:*
.
By Lemma
9
,

\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}},\vec{u}:\mathcal{U};\Psi^{\circ},\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\vdash G^{\prime\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]:*
.
By induction, there exists
G^{\prime}

such that
G^{\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u])

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}},\vec{u}:\mathcal{U},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{0};\Psi^{\circ},\vec{u}:\mathcal{U},\Psi^{\circ}_{0}\vdash G^{\prime}:*
.

•

DW:Pi
.
Then
G=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G^{\prime\prime}
.
We have
\mathsf{NBNF}(G)=G
.

•

DW:App
.
Then
G=G^{\prime\prime}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G^{\prime\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\circ}\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi^{\circ}\vdash U_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}
.
By induction, there exists
G^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}
, and
\Psi^{\circ}_{3}

such that
G^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime})
,
and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}
and
\Psi^{\circ}_{3}
consist of only fresh generators,
and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{3}\vdash G^{\prime\prime\prime}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
By inversion on the graph type formation rules, there are two cases:

(1)

By inversion on
DW:RecPi
,

G^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mu\gamma.\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{0}
.
We have
\mathsf{NBNF}(G)=G
.

(2)

By inversion on
DW:Pi
,

G^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{0}

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3},u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{3},u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}\vdash G_{0}:*
.
By multiple applications of
OM:Gen
,

{\Omega^{\circ}},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}
.
By Lemma
9
,

\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}},{\Omega^{\circ}}_{3};\Psi^{\circ},\Psi^{\circ}_{3}\vdash G_{0}[U_{f},U_{t}/u_{f},u_{t}]:*
.
We have
\mathsf{NBNF}(G)=\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{0}^{\prime\prime}[U_{f},U_{t}/u_{f},u_{t}])
.
Apply induction.

∎

Corollary
11
proves an especially important
property of
\Omega
context splitting: it maintains the affine restriction
on well-typed vertices under
{\Omega^{\circ}}
contexts. If
{\Omega^{\circ}}
splits
into
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}
and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}
(and
{\Omega^{\circ}}
contains at most one
mapping per generator), then the set of VSs
U
where

{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
is completely distinct from
that where
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
, meaning it is
impossible for a future spawned under
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}
to have the same
vertex as a future spawned under
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}
.
In order to prove this, we first prove weaker lemmas that apply only to
VPs (Lemmas
9
and
10
).
We then extend the results to general VSs by formally defining the relation
between VSs and VPs.
Figure
37
defines the judgment
U~\mathsf{normal}

(by way of an auxiliary judgment
U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}
), and
Lemma
6
shows that a VS resulting from evaluation is
normal.
Furthermore (Lemma
7
), any normal VS of VS type
\blacklozenge

is a VP.
Because evaluation preserves typing and any well-formed VS under a context
{\Omega^{\circ}}
may be evaluated to
an equivalent VP, this completes the proof of the corollary.

\frac{}{u~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(VNN:Var)}\frac{}{\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(VNN:Gen)}\frac{U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}{\mathsf{fst}~U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(VNN:Fst)}\frac{U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}{\mathsf{snd}~U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(VNN:Snd)}\frac{U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}}{U~\mathsf{normal}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(VN:VNN)}\frac{U_{1}~\mathsf{normal}\quad U_{2}~\mathsf{normal}}{(U_{1},U_{2})~\mathsf{normal}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(VN:Pair)}

Figure 37
.
Definition of normal vertex structures.

Lemma 0.

If
U\Downarrow U^{\prime}

then
U^{\prime}~\mathsf{normal}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

U\Downarrow U^{\prime}
.

•

UV:Var
.
Then
U^{\prime}=u
.
Apply
VNN:Var
and
VN:VNN
.

•

UV:Path
.
Then
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
Apply
VNN:Gen
and
VN:VNN
.

•

UV:Pair
.
Then
U=(U_{1},U_{2})

and
U^{\prime}=(U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime})

and
U_{1}\Downarrow U_{1}^{\prime}

and
U_{2}\Downarrow U_{2}^{\prime}
.
By induction,

U_{1}^{\prime}~\mathsf{normal}

and
U_{2}^{\prime}~\mathsf{normal}
.
Apply
VN:Pair
.

•

UV:FstPair
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime}

and
U^{\prime\prime}\Downarrow(U^{\prime},U_{0})
.
By induction,

(U^{\prime},U_{0})~\mathsf{normal}
.
By inversion on
VN:Pair

(inversion on
VN:VNN
does not apply since a pair cannot be
~\mathsf{normal^{-}}
).

•

UV:SndPair
.
Similar to previous case.

•

UV:FstNotPair
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime}

and
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
U^{\prime\prime}\Downarrow U^{\prime\prime\prime}

and
U^{\prime\prime\prime}\neq(U_{1},U_{2})
.
By induction,

U^{\prime\prime\prime}~\mathsf{normal}
.
By inversion on
VN:VNN
,

U^{\prime\prime\prime}~\mathsf{normal^{-}}
.
Apply
VNN:Fst
and
VN:VNN
.

•

UV:SndNotPair
.
Similar to previous case.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and either
U~\mathsf{normal}
or
U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}

then
U=\mathit{VP}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

U~\mathsf{normal}
or
U~\mathsf{normal^{-}}
.

•

VNN:Var
.
This case does not apply since it is not possible to type a variable under
{\Omega^{\circ}}
.

•

VNN:Gen
.
Then
U=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.

•

VNN:Fst
.
Then
U=\mathsf{fst}~U^{\prime}

and
U^{\prime}~\mathsf{normal^{-}}
.
By induction,

U^{\prime}=\mathit{VP}^{\prime}
.

•

VNN:Snd
.
Similar to the previous case.

•

VN:VNN
.
Apply induction.

•

VN:Pair
.
By inversion on the VS typing rules,
a pair cannot be given the type
\blacklozenge
,
so this case does not apply.

∎

Lemma 0.

For every
\mathit{VP}
, there exists a
\vec{u}
such that
if
{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
, then
there exists a
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
such
that
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in{\Omega^{\circ}}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathit{VP}
.

•

\mathit{VP}=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}^{\prime})
.
Let
\vec{u}
=
\vec{u}^{\prime}
.
If
{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}
,
then by inversion on
U:Subtype
and
U:OmegaGen
,

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in{\Omega^{\circ}}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
U:OmegaGen
and
U:Subtype
.

•

\mathit{VP}=\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}^{\prime}
.
By induction, there exists a
\vec{u}
such that
if
{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
, then
there exists a
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
such
that
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in{\Omega^{\circ}}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
.
If
{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
,
then by inversion on
U:Subtype
and
U:Fst
,

{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Therefore,
there exists a
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
such
that
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in{\Omega^{\circ}}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{a}
.
Apply
U:Fst
and
U:Subtype
.

•

\mathit{VP}=\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}^{\prime}
.
Similar to the previous case.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
,
then there is no
\mathit{VP}
such that

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
and

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

•

US:Prod
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\square}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\blacksquare}
.
Consider possible prefixes of
\mathit{VP}
.

–

\mathit{VP}=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
. This would not have the type
\blacklozenge
.

–

\mathit{VP}
begins with
\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
This is not possible because it is not possible to derive

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.

–

\mathit{VP}
begins with
\mathsf{snd}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
Symmetric to above.

•

US:SplitBoth
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\prime\blacksquare}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}

where
\mathcal{U}_{a}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime}

and
\mathcal{U}_{b}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\prime\prime}
.
Consider possible prefixes of
\mathit{VP}
.

–

\mathit{VP}=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
. This would not have the type
\blacklozenge
.

–

\mathit{VP}
begins with
\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
We have
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime}
.
Replacing
\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
in
\mathit{VP}

with
\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
gives us derivations

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
,
which is a contradiction by induction.

–

\mathit{VP}
begins with
\mathsf{snd}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
Symmetric to above.

•

US:SplitLeft
.
Then
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{a}

and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{b}^{\square}

where
\mathcal{U}_{a}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime}
.
Consider possible prefixes of
\mathit{VP}
.

–

\mathit{VP}=\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
. This would not have the type
\blacklozenge
.

–

\mathit{VP}
begins with
\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
We have
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime}
.
Replacing
\mathsf{fst}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
in
\mathit{VP}

with
\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
gives us derivations

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{a}^{\prime\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
,
which is a contradiction by induction.

–

\mathit{VP}
begins with
\mathsf{snd}~\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})
.
This is not possible because it is not possible to derive

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.

•

US:SplitRight
. Symmetric to above.

•

US:Corecursive
. By induction.

•

US:Subtype
.
Then
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}
.
If a VP existed such that

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
,
then
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
must be true
in order to derive
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
By inversion,
\mathcal{U}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
so
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
by
UT:Transitive
.
By
U:Subtype
,
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
so
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
,
which is a contradiction by induction.

•

US:Commutative
. By induction.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

then there is no
\mathit{VP}
such that

{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
and

{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.

Proof.

Suppose that

{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
and

{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
By Lemma
8
, there exists
\vec{u}
such that

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1}\in{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2}\in{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

and
\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
and

\vec{u}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
By Lemmas
1
and
5
,
there exists
\mathcal{U}

such that
\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{2}

(one case requires an application of
US:Commutative
).
This contradicts Lemma
9
.
∎

Corollary 0.

If
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

this contradicts our assumption that the domain of
{\Omega^{\circ}}
contains no duplicates.

Proof.

By Lemmas
1
–
3
, there
exists
U^{\prime}
such that
U\Downarrow U^{\prime}

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge
.
By Lemmas
6
and
7
,
U^{\prime}=\mathit{VP}
.
This contradicts Lemma
10
,
given our assumption that
{\Omega^{\circ}}
contains
no duplicate generators.
∎

In the statement of Lemma
12
, which is the soundness
result for graph type expansion, we use the function
\mathit{Verts(}\cdot\mathit{)}

to get the set of vertices of a graph.
Formally,
\mathit{Verts(}(V,E,s,t)\mathit{)}=V
.

Lemma 0.

If
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G:*

and
G
is in NBNF
and
g\in\mathit{Exp}(G)
,
then
g
is a well-formed graph
and for all
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g\mathit{)}
,
either
{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

or
U
is a fresh vertex.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation
of
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G:*
.

•

DW:Empty
.
Then
G=\bullet
.
By inversion,
g=\bullet

and
\mathit{Verts(}g\mathit{)}=\{u\}
, where
u~\mathsf{fresh}
.

•

DW:Seq
.
Then
G=G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\cdot;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By inversion,
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are in NBNF.
By inversion,
g=g_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}g_{2}

where
g_{1}\in\mathit{Exp}(G_{1})

and
g_{2}\in\mathit{Exp}(G_{2})
.
By induction,
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are well-formed graphs
and for all non-fresh vertex paths
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g_{1}\mathit{)}
,
we have
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and for all non-fresh vertex paths
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g_{2}\mathit{)}
,
we have
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By Corollary
11
,
there is no
U
such that
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
Without loss of generality, the sets of fresh vertices in
g_{1}

and
g_{2}
are disjoint from each other and the
contexts, so
\mathit{Verts(}g_{1}\mathit{)}

and
\mathit{Verts(}g_{2}\mathit{)}
are disjoint,
and so
g
is a well-formed graph.
For all non-fresh vertex paths
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g\mathit{)}
,
either
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g_{1}\mathit{)}
or
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g_{2}\mathit{)}

and so by Lemma
6
,

{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.

•

DW:Or
.
Then
G=G_{1}\lor G_{2}

and
\Delta;{\Omega^{\circ}};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G_{1}:*

and
\Delta;{\Omega^{\circ}};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G_{2}:*
.
By inversion,
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are in NBNF.
By inversion,
g\in\mathit{Exp}(G_{1})

or
g\in\mathit{Exp}(G_{2})
.
Apply induction in both cases.

•

DW:Spawn
.
Then
G=G^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}}

and
\Delta;{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\Psi^{\circ}\vdash G^{\prime}:*

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U^{\prime}:\blacklozenge

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}\rightsquigarrow{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1}\boxplus{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2}
.
By inversion,
G^{\prime}
is in NBNF.
By inversion,
g=g^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}}

where
g^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(G^{\prime})
.
By induction,
g^{\prime}
is a well-formed graph
and for all non-fresh vertex paths
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g^{\prime}\mathit{)}
,
we have
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
By Corollary
11
,
there is no
U
such that
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{1};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and
{\Omega^{\circ}}_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.
Therefore,
U^{\prime}\not\in\mathit{Verts(}g^{\prime}\mathit{)}
.
Thus,
g
is a well-formed graph.
For all non-fresh vertex paths
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g\mathit{)}
,
either
U\in\mathit{Verts(}g^{\prime}\mathit{)}

or
U=U^{\prime}
and so by Lemma
6
,

{\Omega^{\circ}};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.

•

DW:Touch
.
Then
G=\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}
.
By inversion,
g=\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}

and
\mathit{Verts(}g\mathit{)}=\{u\}
, where
u~\mathsf{fresh}
.

•

DW:New
.
Then
G=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime}
.
By inversion,
G
is not in NBNF, so this case does not apply.

•

DW:Pi
,
DW:RecPi
.
These cases do not apply because the graph kind is
*
.

•

DW:App
.
Then
G=G^{\prime}[U_{f};U_{t}]
.
This contradicts
g\in\mathit{Exp}(G)

since
\mathit{Exp}(G)=\emptyset
,
so this case does not apply.

∎

Proof of Theorem
1
:

Proof.

Follows directly from Lemmas
4
,

5

and
12
.
∎

\frac{G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}}{G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Seq1)}\frac{G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{2}^{\prime}}{G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Seq2)}\frac{G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}}{G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Par1)}\frac{G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{2}^{\prime}}{G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Par2)}\frac{G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}}{G_{1}\lor G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}\lor G_{2}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Or1)}\frac{G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{2}^{\prime}}{G_{1}\lor G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}\lor G_{2}^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Or2)}\frac{G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}}{G\!\sswarrow_{U}\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Future)}\frac{}{\mu\gamma.G\hookrightarrow G[\mu\gamma.G/\gamma]}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Rec)}\frac{G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}}{\Pi[u_{f};u_{t}].G\hookrightarrow\Pi[u_{f};u_{t}].G^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:Pi)}\frac{G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}}{G[U_{f};U_{t}]\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}[U_{f};U_{t}]}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:App)}\frac{G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}}{\mathsf{new}~u.G\hookrightarrow\mathsf{new}~u.G^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{\footnotesize(UR:New)}

Figure 38
.
Graph type unrolling.

Proof of Theorem
2
:

To prove the theorem, we must first state and prove two technical lemmas about
graph type unrolling, whose full rules are given in Figure
38
.
The first, Lemma
13
, is a “framing” property that allows us to unroll part of a graph
type multiple times while keeping the rest of the graph type the same.
The second, Lemma
14
, allows us to commute unrolling and substitution of VS variables.

Lemma 0.

(1)

If
G_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}

then
G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}
.

(2)

If
G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{2}^{\prime}

then
G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}^{\prime}
.

(3)

If
G_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}

then
G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}
.

(4)

If
G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{2}^{\prime}

then
G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}G_{2}^{\prime}
.

(5)

If
G_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}

then
G_{1}\lor G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}\lor G_{2}
.

(6)

If
G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{2}^{\prime}

then
G_{1}\lor G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}\lor G_{2}^{\prime}
.

(7)

If
G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}

then
G\!\sswarrow_{U}\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}
.

(8)

If
G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}

then
\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}].G\hookrightarrow^{*}\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}].G^{\prime}
.

(9)

If
G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}

then
G[U_{f};U_{t}]\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}[U_{f};U_{t}]
.

(10)

If
G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}

then
\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G\hookrightarrow^{*}\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on
G_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}
.

•

G_{1}=G_{1}^{\prime}
.
Therefore,

G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{0}G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}
.

•

G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}
.
By induction,

G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}
.
By
UR:Seq1
,

G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}
.
Therefore,

G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}
.

(2)

All cases are similar.

∎

Lemma 0.

(1)

If
G[U/u]\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}

then there exists a
G^{\prime\prime}

such that
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime\prime}

and
G^{\prime\prime}[U/u]=G^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
G[U/u]\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}

then there exists a
G^{\prime\prime}

such that
G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime}

and
G^{\prime\prime}[U/u]=G^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on
G[U/u]\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
.

•

UR:Rec
.
Then
G^{\prime}=G_{1}[U/u][\mu\gamma.G_{1}[U/u]/\gamma]=G_{1}[\mu\gamma.G_{1}/\gamma][U/u]

and
G[U/u]=\mu\gamma.G_{1}[U/u]

and
G=\mu\gamma.G_{1}
.
By
UR:Rec
,

G\hookrightarrow G_{1}[\mu\gamma.G_{1}/\gamma]
.

•

UR:Seq1
.
Then
G[U/u]=G_{1}[U/u]\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}[U/u]

and
G=G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}[U/u]

and
G_{1}[U/u]\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists a
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}

such that
G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}[U/u]=G_{1}^{\prime}
.
Therefore,

G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime\prime}[U/u]\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}[U/u]=(G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2})[U/u]
.
By
UR:Seq1
,

G\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}
.

•

UR:Future
.
Then
G[U/u]=(G_{1}[U/u])\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}[U/u]}

and
G=G_{1}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}}

and
G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}[U/u]}

and
G_{1}[U/u]\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists a
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}

such that
G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}[U/u]\\
=G_{1}^{\prime}
.
Therefore,

G^{\prime}=G_{1}^{\prime\prime}[U/u]\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}[U/u]}=(G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}})[U/u]
.
By
UR:Future
,

G\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U^{\prime}}
.

(2)

By induction on
G[U/u]\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}
.

•

G[U/u]=G^{\prime}
.
Therefore
G^{\prime\prime}=G
.

•

G[U/u]\hookrightarrow G_{1}
and
G_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}
.
By part
1
,
there exists a
G_{1}^{\prime}

such that
G\hookrightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}

and
G_{1}^{\prime}[U/u]=G_{1}
.
Therefore

G_{1}^{\prime}[U/u]\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists a
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}

such that
G_{1}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
G_{1}^{\prime\prime}[U/u]=G^{\prime}
.
Therefore

G\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

We now prove the soundness theorem.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

By induction on the derivation of

e\Downarrow v\mid g
.

•

C:App
.
Let
F=\mathsf{fun}[u_{f};u_{t}]~f~x=e^{\prime}
.
Then
e={e_{1}[U_{f};U_{t}]}~e_{2}

and
g=g_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}g_{2}\mathbin{\oplus}g_{3}

and
e_{1}\Downarrow F\mid g_{1}

and
e_{2}\Downarrow v^{\prime}\mid g_{2}

and

e^{\prime}[F/f][U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}][v^{\prime}/x]\Downarrow v\mid g_{3}

By inversion on
S:Type-Eq
and
S:App
,

\cdot;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau\equiv\tau_{2}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]::\mathsf{Ty}

and

G=G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{3}[U_{f};U_{t}]

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega^{\prime}

and
\Omega^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{2}\boxplus\Omega_{3}

and

\cdot;\Omega_{1};\Psi;\cdot\vdash e_{1}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G_{3}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}\mid G_{1}

and
\cdot;\Omega_{2};\Psi;\cdot\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{1}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid G_{2}

and
\Omega_{3};\cdot\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}
.
By induction,
there exists
\Psi^{\circ}_{1}
such that
\vec{u}\in\Psi^{\circ}_{1}
implies
\vec{u}~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1};\cdot\vdash F:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G_{3}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}\mid\bullet

and there exists
\Psi^{\circ}_{2}
such that
\vec{u}\in\Psi^{\circ}_{2}
implies
\vec{u}~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{2};\cdot\vdash v^{\prime}:\tau_{1}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid\bullet

and there exists
G_{1}^{\prime}
such that
G_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}
and
g_{1}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime}))

and there exists
G_{2}^{\prime}
such that
G_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{2}^{\prime}
and
g_{2}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2}^{\prime}))
.
By inversion on
S:Fun
,

G_{3}=\mu\gamma.\Pi[u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}

and

\Delta;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1},u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};f:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2},x:\tau_{1}\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau_{2}\mid G_{4}

where
\Delta=\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*

and

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1},u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1},u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

By Lemma

,

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1};\cdot\vdash\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G_{3}[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

By inversion on
K:Fun
,

\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1}\vdash G_{3}:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
By weakening and 5 applications of Lemma
9
,

\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1},\Psi^{\circ}_{2};\cdot\vdash e^{\prime}[F/f][U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}][v^{\prime}/x]:\tau_{2}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid G_{4}[G_{3}/\gamma][U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]
.
(Since
\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1},u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

then
\tau_{1}[G_{3}/\gamma]\\
=\tau_{1}
, and similar for
\tau_{2}
.)

By induction,
there exists
\Psi^{\circ}_{0}
such that
\vec{u}\in\Psi^{\circ}_{0}
implies
\vec{u}~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}_{1},\Psi^{\circ}_{2},\Psi^{\circ}_{0};\cdot\vdash v:\tau_{2}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\mid\bullet

and there exists
G_{4}^{\prime}
such that
G_{4}[G_{3}/\gamma][U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}]\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{4}^{\prime}
and

Apply weakening and
S:Type-Eq
.

,

,

(\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}[G_{3}/\gamma])[U_{f};U_{t}]\hookrightarrow^{*}(\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}^{\prime\prime})[U_{f};U_{t}]

UR:Rec
,

G_{3}[U_{f};U_{t}]\hookrightarrow(\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}[G_{3}/\gamma])[U_{f};U_{t}]
, so

By 3 applications of Lemma

,

G_{1}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}\mathbin{\oplus}(G_{3}[U_{f};U_{t}])\hookrightarrow^{*}G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}(\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}^{\prime\prime})[U_{f};U_{t}]

We have
\mathsf{NBNF}((\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}^{\prime\prime})[U_{f};U_{t}])=\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{4}^{\prime\prime}[U_{f}/u_{f}][U_{t}/u_{t}])=\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{4}^{\prime})
. Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{l l }&\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}(\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}^{\prime\prime})[U_{f};U_{t}])\\
=&\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2}^{\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathsf{NBNF}((\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}^{\prime\prime})[U_{f};U_{t}])\\
=&\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2}^{\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{4}^{\prime})\end{array}

We have

\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2}^{\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{4}^{\prime}))\\
=&\{g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}g_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}g_{3}^{\prime}\mid g_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime})),g_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{2}^{\prime})),g_{3}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{4}^{\prime}))\}\end{array}

Therefore,
g\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}G_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}(\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].G_{4}^{\prime\prime})[U_{f};U_{t}]))
.

C:Pair
.
Then

,

Therefore,

C:Future
.
Then

G=G^{\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}

and
\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}

and
\cdot;\Omega_{1};\Psi;\cdot\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}\mid G^{\prime\prime}

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash U:\blacklozenge

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash U:\blacklozenge
.

By induction,
there exists
\Psi^{\circ}
such that
\vec{u}\in\Psi^{\circ}
implies
\vec{u}~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash v^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}\mid\bullet

and there exists
G^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
G^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
g^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}))
.

By Lemma
2
,

\Psi\vdash U\equiv\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
By Lemma
10
and weakening,

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}\vdash\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
By
S:Handle
,

\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash v:\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[\mathit{VP}]\mid\bullet
.

By
CE:Reflexive
and
CE:Fut
,

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U]\equiv\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[\mathit{VP}]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply weakening and
S:Type-Eq
twice.

By Lemma
13
,

G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}
.

We have

\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U})=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime})\!\sswarrow_{\mathit{VP}}

and

\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime})\!\sswarrow_{\mathit{VP}})=\{g^{\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{\mathit{VP}}\mid g^{\prime\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}))\}

Therefore,
g\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\sswarrow_{U}))
.

•

C:Touch
.
Then
e=\mathsf{touch}~e^{\prime}

and
g=g^{\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{\mathit{VP}}\!\ssearrow}

and
e^{\prime}\Downarrow\mathsf{handle}[\mathit{VP}]~v\mid g^{\prime}
.

By inversion on
S:Type-Eq
and
S:Touch
,

\cdot;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau\equiv\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
G=G^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}

and

\cdot;\Omega;\Psi;\cdot\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U]\mid G^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,
there exists
\Psi^{\circ}
such that
\vec{u}\in\Psi^{\circ}
implies
\vec{u}~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{handle}[\mathit{VP}]~v:\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U]\mid\bullet

and there exists
G^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
G^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
g^{\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}))
.
By inversion on
S:Type-Eq
and
S:Handle
,

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[U]\equiv\tau^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{future}[\mathit{VP}]::\mathsf{Ty}

and

\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash\tau^{\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and

\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash v:\tau^{\prime\prime}\mid\bullet

Apply weakening and
S:Type-Eq
twice.

By inversion on
CE:Fut
,

\Psi,\Psi^{\circ}\vdash U\equiv\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
By
UV:Path
and Lemma
3
,

U\Downarrow\mathit{VP}
.

By Lemma
13
,

G\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}
.
We have

\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow})=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{\mathit{VP}}\!\ssearrow}

and

\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime})\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{\mathit{VP}}\!\ssearrow})=\{g^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{\mathit{VP}}\!\ssearrow}\mid g^{\prime\prime}\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}))\}

Therefore,
g\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathbin{\oplus}\mathop{{}^{U}\!\ssearrow}))
.

•

C:New
.
Then
e=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.e^{\prime}

and
e^{\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]\Downarrow v\mid g

and
\vec{u}^{\prime}~\mathsf{fresh}
.

By inversion on
S:Type-Eq
and
S:New
,

\cdot;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau\equiv\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
G=\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime\prime}

and

\cdot;\Omega,u:\mathcal{U};\Psi,u:\mathcal{U};\cdot\vdash e^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}\mid G^{\prime\prime}

By
U:OmegaGen
,

\vec{u}:\mathcal{U};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}
.

By
U:PsiGen
,

\cdot;\Psi,\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\vdash\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u}):\mathcal{U}
.

By
OM:Gen
,

\Omega,\vec{u}:\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\Omega\boxplus\vec{u}^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}
.

By weakening and Lemma
9
,

\cdot;\Omega,\vec{u}:\mathcal{U};\Psi,\vec{u}:\mathcal{U};\cdot\vdash e^{\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]:\tau^{\prime}\mid G^{\prime\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]
.
(Since
u
is local to
e
and
G
,

\tau^{\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]=\tau^{\prime}
).

By induction,
there exists
\Psi^{\circ}
such that
\vec{u}\in\Psi^{\circ}
implies
\vec{u}~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\Psi,\vec{u}:\mathcal{U},\Psi^{\circ};\cdot\vdash v:\tau^{\prime}\mid\bullet

and there exists
G^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
G^{\prime\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
g\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime}))
.

Apply weakening and
S:Type-Eq
.

By Lemma
14
,

\exists G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}.G^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\wedge G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u]=G^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

By Lemma
13
,

G\hookrightarrow^{*}\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
.

We have

\mathsf{NBNF}(\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime})=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}[\mathsf{gen}(\vec{u})/u])=\mathsf{NBNF}(G^{\prime\prime\prime})

Therefore,
g\in\mathit{Exp}(\mathsf{NBNF}(\mathsf{new}~u\!:\!\mathcal{U}.G^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}))
.

Figure 41.
Typing rules for unannotated values.

As stated in the main body of this paper, the main result of this section
requires an affine typing judgement for (annotated) values,
a kinding judgement for unannotated types, and a typing judgement for unannotated values,
whose rules are given in Figures
39
,
40
, and
41
respectively.

Before showing the main result of the section, we state and prove a lemma
about how the two annotation judgments commute with substitution.
Lemma
1
allows us to substitute any VS type for
a free VS type variable in the type annotation judgement.
This lemma is required for the proof of the main theorem, but is also useful
for thinking about annotating polymorphic types.
For example, to annotate a type
\alpha~\mathsf{list}
,
we can add
\alpha
to the context with
kind
t\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
for a fresh vertex structure type
variable
t
.
The annotated type and vertex structure type will refer to
t
.
If we instantiate
\alpha
with a concrete type
\sigma^{\prime}
,
the resulting annotated type and vertex structure type will strongly depend on
the particular type
\sigma^{\prime}
.
For example, no vertices need to be added if
\sigma^{\prime}=\mathsf{int}
, but we
need more vertices if
\sigma^{\prime}=\mathsf{int}~\mathsf{future}
.
We also prove Lemma
2
,
which states that well-formed unannotated types maintain their kinds
when substituting any free type variables appropriately.

Lemma 0.

If
\Upsilon\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\tau;\mathcal{U}

then
\Upsilon[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\vdash_{U}\sigma[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\leadsto\tau[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];\mathcal{U}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation
of
\Upsilon\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\tau;\mathcal{U}
.

•

F:TyVar
.
Then
\sigma=\alpha

and
\tau={U}~

and
\Upsilon=\Upsilon^{\prime},\alpha::\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
We have
\Upsilon[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]=\Upsilon^{\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t],\alpha::\mathcal{U}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
F:TyVar
.

•

F:Unit
.
Then
\sigma=\tau=\mathsf{unit}

and
\mathcal{U}=\blacklozenge
.
Apply
F:Unit
.

•

F:Fun
.
Then
\sigma=\tau=\Pi[u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t};u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G}\tau_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}=\blacklozenge
.
We have
\sigma[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]=\tau[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]=\sigma=\tau=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]].\tau_{1}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\xrightarrow{G[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]}\tau_{2}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
.
Apply
F:Fun
.

•

F:Prod
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}

and
\tau=\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Upsilon\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma_{1}\leadsto\tau_{1};\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Upsilon\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~U}\sigma_{2}\leadsto\tau_{2};\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma_{1}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\leadsto\tau_{1}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];\mathcal{U}_{1}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]

and
\Upsilon[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~U}\sigma_{2}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\leadsto\tau_{2}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];\mathcal{U}_{2}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
.
Apply
F:Prod
.

•

F:Sum
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

F:Fut
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}

and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[\mathsf{snd}~U]

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\blacksquare}\times\blacklozenge^{\blacksquare}

and
\Upsilon\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma^{\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\leadsto\tau^{\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
.
Apply
F:Fut
.

•

F:Rec
.
Then
\sigma=\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}

and
\tau=\mu(\alpha;u:\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}.\tau^{\prime};U)

and
\mathcal{U}=\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}/t^{\prime}]
.
By induction,

\Upsilon[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t],\alpha::\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u}\sigma^{\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\leadsto\tau^{\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t][\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]/t^{\prime}]

(since
t^{\prime}
is bound within
\mathcal{U}
,
t\neq t^{\prime}
).
Apply
F:Rec
.
