Lemma 0.

If
\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

then
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma::\mathsf{Ty}
.

•

KU:Unit
.
Then
\sigma=\mathsf{unit}
.
Apply
KU:Unit
.

•

KU:Var
.
Then
\sigma=\alpha^{\prime}
.
There are two cases:

(1)

\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
.
Then
\Upsilon=\Upsilon^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
KU:Var
.

(2)

\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
.
Then
\sigma[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]=\sigma^{\prime}
.
True by premise.

•

KU:Fun
.
Then
\sigma=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].c_{1}\xrightarrow{G[u_{f};u_{t}]}c_{2}

and
\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash c_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash c_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Therefore,
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
contain no instances of
\alpha
,
so
\sigma[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]=\sigma

Apply
KU:Fun

•

KU:Prod
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon\vdash\sigma_{1}[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma_{2}[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
KU:Prod
.

•

KU:Sum
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

KU:Fut
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{future}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon\vdash\sigma^{\prime\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
F:Fut
.

•

KU:Rec
.
Then
\sigma=\mu\alpha^{\prime}.\sigma^{\prime\prime}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty},\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma^{\prime\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma^{\prime\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
KU:Rec
.

∎

The main result of this section additionally requires the ability to commute both recursive type annotation and recursive type kinding with the unrolling of recursive types. Lemma
3
allows us to do this: loosely speaking, if a type
\sigma
annotates to a well-formed
\tau
where
\sigma
and
\tau
are the bodies of recursive types
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime}
respectively, then the unrolling of
\sigma^{\prime}
annotates to a well-formed unrolling of
\tau^{\prime}
. Note that the statement of Lemma
3
is stronger than this description.

Lemma 0.

If
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\tau;\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}/t]

and
\mathsf{Unann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma::\mathsf{Ty}
,
then
\Upsilon\vdash_{U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau^{\prime\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
,
and for any
\Psi
such that

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
,
it follows that
\cdot;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash\tau^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation
of
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\tau;\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.

•

F:TyVar
.
Then
\sigma=\alpha^{\prime}

and
\tau=\alpha^{\prime}~U
.
There are two cases:

(1)

\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
.
Then
\Upsilon=\Upsilon^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
.
We have
\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]=\sigma

and
\tau[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]=\alpha^{\prime}~(U[U^{\prime}/u])
.
By
F:TyVar
,

\Upsilon\vdash_{U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma\leadsto\\
\alpha^{\prime}~(U[U^{\prime}/u]);\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By
K:Var
and
K:App
,

\cdot;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash\alpha^{\prime}~(U[U^{\prime}/u])::\mathsf{Ty}

for all
\Psi
such that

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
CE:Reflexive
.

(2)

\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
.
Then
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\nu t.\mathcal{U}
.
We have
\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]=\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}

and
\tau[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]=(\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime}))~(U[U^{\prime}/u])
.
By
F:Rec
,

\Upsilon\vdash_{U[U^{\prime}/u]}\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};U[U^{\prime}/u]);\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By
U:PsiVar
,

\cdot;u^{\prime}:\nu t.\mathcal{U}\vdash u^{\prime}:\nu t.\mathcal{U}
.
By weakening and
K:Rec
,

\cdot;\Psi,u^{\prime}:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon\vdash\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})::\mathsf{Ty}

for all
\Psi
such that

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
CE:BetaEq
,

\cdot;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash(\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime}))~(U[U^{\prime}/u])\equiv\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};U[U^{\prime}/u])::\mathsf{Ty}

(since
u^{\prime}
is a fresh binding,
\tau^{\prime}
contains no instances of
u^{\prime}
).
Apply
CE:Commutative
.

•

F:Unit
.
Then
\sigma=\tau=\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]=\tau[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]=\mathsf{unit}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\blacklozenge
.
Apply
F:Unit
.
By
K:Unit
and
CE:Reflexive
,

\cdot;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash\mathsf{unit}\equiv\mathsf{unit}::\mathsf{Ty}

for all
\Psi
.

•

F:Fun
.
Then
\sigma=\tau=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G}\tau_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\blacklozenge
.
By inversion on
KU:Fun
,

\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;\cdot;\cdot\vdash G:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*
.
Therefore

\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
, and
G
contain no instances of
u
or
\alpha
,
so we have
\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]=\tau[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]=\Pi[u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t};u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G}\tau_{2}
.
Apply
F:Fun
.
By
K:Fun
and
CE:Reflexive
and weakening,

\cdot;\Psi;\Upsilon\vdash\Pi[u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t};u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G}\tau_{2}\equiv\Pi[u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t};u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G}\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}

for all
\Psi
.

•

F:Prod
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}

and
\tau=\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma_{1}\leadsto\tau_{1};\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~U}\sigma_{2}\leadsto\tau_{2};\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By inversion on
KU:Prod
,

\mathsf{Unann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\mathsf{Unann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma_{1}[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau_{1}^{\prime\prime};\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Upsilon\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma_{2}[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime};\mathcal{U}_{2}
,
and for any
\Psi_{1}
such that

\cdot;\Psi_{1}\vdash\mathsf{fst}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi_{1},u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
,

\cdot;\Psi_{1};\Upsilon\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau_{1}[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
,
and for any
\Psi_{2}
such that

\cdot;\Psi_{2}\vdash\mathsf{snd}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{2}

and
\cdot;\Psi_{2},u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
,

\cdot;\Psi_{1};\Upsilon\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau_{2}[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
F:Prod
,

\Upsilon\vdash_{U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau_{1}^{\prime\prime}\times\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By
U:Fst
and
U:Snd
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mathsf{fst}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mathsf{snd}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{2}

for all
\Psi
such that

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
CE:Prod
.

F:Sum
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

F:Fut
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{0}~\mathsf{future}

and
\tau=\tau_{0}~\mathsf{future}[\mathsf{snd}~U]

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\blacksquare}\times\blacklozenge^{\blacksquare}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma_{0}\leadsto\tau_{0};\mathcal{U}_{0}
.

,

\mathsf{Unann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma_{0}::\mathsf{Ty}
.

,
and for any
\Psi_{0}
such that

\cdot;\Psi_{0}\vdash\mathsf{fst}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{0}

and
\cdot;\Psi_{0},u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
,

\cdot;\Psi_{0};\Upsilon\vdash\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau_{0}[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
F:Fut
,

\Upsilon\vdash_{U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{future}[\mathsf{snd}~U[U^{\prime}/u]];\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By
U:Fst
and
U:Snd
,

\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mathsf{fst}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}_{0}

and
\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mathsf{snd}~U[U^{\prime}/u]:\blacklozenge

for all
\Psi
such that

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.

UE:Reflexive
and
CE:Fut
.

•

F:Rec
.
Then
\sigma=\mu\alpha^{\prime}.\sigma_{0}

and
\tau=\mu(\alpha^{\prime};u_{0}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\tau_{0};U)

and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\nu t^{\prime}.\mathcal{U}_{0}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty},\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u_{0}}\sigma_{0}\leadsto\tau_{0};\mathcal{U}_{0}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t^{\prime}]
.
By inversion on
KU:Rec
,

\mathsf{Unann}(\Upsilon),\alpha^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty},\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma_{0}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,

\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u_{0}}\sigma_{0}[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime};\mathcal{U}_{0}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t^{\prime}]
,
and for any
\Psi_{0}
such that

and

,

\cdot;\Psi_{0};\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau_{0}[U^{\prime}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By
F:Rec
,

\Upsilon\vdash_{U[U^{\prime}/u]}\sigma[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\mu(\alpha^{\prime};u_{0}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime};U[U^{\prime}/u]);\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By
U:PsiVar
and
UT:Corec1
and
U:Subtype
and weakening,

\cdot;\Psi,u_{0}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash u_{0}:\mathcal{U}_{0}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t^{\prime}]

and
\cdot;\Psi,u_{0}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime},u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty},\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}

for all
\Psi
such that

\cdot;\Psi\vdash U[U^{\prime}/u]:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U};\Upsilon,\alpha^{\prime}::\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty},\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
UE:Reflexive
and
CE:Rec
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma::\mathsf{Ty}
.

•

KU:Unit
.
Then
\sigma=\mathsf{unit}
.
By
F:Unit
,

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\mathsf{unit};\blacklozenge

for all
U
.

•

KU:Var
.
Then
\sigma=\alpha
.
and
\Upsilon=\Upsilon^{\prime},\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Thus

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)=\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon^{\prime}),\alpha::t\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}
where
t~\mathsf{fresh}
.
By
F:TyVar
,

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\alpha~U;t

for all
U
.

•

KU:Fun
.
Then
\sigma=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].c_{1}\xrightarrow{G[u_{f};u_{t}]}c_{2}
.
By
F:Fun
,

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].c_{1}\xrightarrow{G[u_{f};u_{t}]}c_{2};\blacklozenge

for all
U
.

•

KU:Prod
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}

and
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,
for all
U

there exists
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
,
\mathcal{U}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{U}_{2}

such that
\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma_{1}\leadsto\tau_{1};\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~U}\sigma_{2}\leadsto\tau_{2};\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
F:Prod
.

•

KU:Sum
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

•

KU:Fut
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}

and
\Upsilon\vdash\sigma^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,
for all
U

there exists
\tau^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

such that
\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~U}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
Apply
F:Fut
.

•

KU:Rec
.
Then
\sigma=\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}

and
\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Thus

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon,\alpha::\mathsf{Ty})=\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::t\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}

where
t~\mathsf{fresh}
.
By induction,
there exists
\tau^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

such that
\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::t\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
1
,

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon),\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]

(since
t~\mathsf{fresh}
,

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]=\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)

and
\sigma^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]\\
=\sigma^{\prime}
).
By
F:Rec
,

\mathsf{Ann}(\Upsilon)\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\mu(\alpha;u:\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\tau^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t];U);\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

for all
U
.

(2)

By induction on the derivation
of
\Upsilon\vdash_{U}\sigma\leadsto\tau;\mathcal{U}
.

F:TyVar
.

\tau=\alpha~U

and

and

,

\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash c_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and

and

K:Fun
and weakening.

F:Prod
.

.

.

F:Sum
.
By symmetry with the previous case.

F:Future
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}

and

and

(3)

By induction on the derivation
of
\cdot\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\sigma\leadsto\tau;\mathcal{U}
.

.

,

.

SV:Unit

for all
\Omega

F:Fun
.
Then
\sigma=\tau=\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{G}\tau_{2}
.
By inversion on
SU:Fun
,

\epsilon=\mathsf{fun}[u_{f};u_{t}]~f~x=e

and
G=(\mu\gamma.\Pi[u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t}].G^{\prime})[u_{f};u_{t}]

and
\gamma~\mathsf{fresh}

and
\gamma:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].*;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};f:\Pi[u_{f}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{f};u_{t}\!:\!\mathcal{U}_{t}].\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma[u_{f};u_{t}]}\tau_{2},x:\tau_{1}\vdash e:\tau_{2}\mid G^{\prime}

and
\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot;u_{f}:\mathcal{U}_{f},u_{t}:\mathcal{U}_{t};\cdot\vdash\tau_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.

,

\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\epsilon\leadsto\mathsf{fun}[u_{f};u_{t}]~f~x=e
.

for all
\Omega
.

F:Prod
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}

and
\tau=\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\cdot\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}}\sigma_{1}\leadsto\tau_{1};\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}}\sigma_{2}\leadsto\tau_{2};\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

SU:Pair
,

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})

and
\vdash\epsilon_{1}:\sigma_{1}

and
\vdash\epsilon_{2}:\sigma_{2}
.

KU:Prod
,

\cdot\vdash\sigma_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}

and
\cdot\vdash\sigma_{2}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,
there exist

and
v_{2}

such that
\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}}\epsilon_{1}\leadsto v_{1}

and
\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}}\epsilon_{2}\leadsto v_{2}
,
and for all
\Omega_{1}
such that
\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{1}
,
we have
\Omega_{1}\vdash_{A}v_{1}:\tau_{1}
,
and for all
\Omega_{2}
such that
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
,
we have
\Omega_{2}\vdash_{A}v_{2}:\tau_{2}
.

FE:Pair
,

\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\epsilon\leadsto(v_{1},v_{2})
.
By
US:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}
.
By Lemma
7
,
there exists
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
such that

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
and

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\square}
and

\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\square}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

for all
\Omega
such that
\Omega;\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
.
By
U:Fst
and
U:Snd
,

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
Apply
SV:Pair
.

•

F:Sum
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}

and
\tau=\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\blacksquare}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\blacksquare}

and
\cdot\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}}\sigma_{1}\leadsto\tau_{1};\mathcal{U}_{1}

and
\cdot\vdash_{\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}}\sigma_{2}\leadsto\tau_{2};\mathcal{U}_{2}
.
By inversion on either
SU:InL
or
SU:InR
,
either
\epsilon=\mathsf{inl}~\epsilon^{\prime}

and
\vdash\epsilon^{\prime}:\sigma_{1}

or
\epsilon=\mathsf{inr}~\epsilon^{\prime}

and
\vdash\epsilon^{\prime}:\sigma_{2}
.
We take the former case, the other is similar.
By inversion on
KU:Sum
,

\cdot\vdash\sigma_{1}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,
there exists
v^{\prime}

such that
\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}}\epsilon^{\prime}\leadsto v^{\prime}
,
and for all
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{1}
,
we have
\Omega^{\prime}\vdash_{A}v^{\prime}:\tau_{1}
.
By
FE:InL
,

\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\epsilon\leadsto\mathsf{inl}~v^{\prime}
.
By
U:Fst
,

\Omega;\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}_{1}

for all
\Omega
such that
\Omega;\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
.
Apply
SV:InL
.

•

F:Future
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}

and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}~\mathsf{future}[\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}]

and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\blacklozenge^{\blacksquare}

and
\cdot\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
SU:Handle
,

\epsilon=\mathsf{handle}~\epsilon^{\prime}

and
\vdash\epsilon^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}
.
By inversion on
KU:Fut
,

\cdot\vdash\sigma^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,
there exists
v^{\prime}

such that
\vdash_{\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}}\epsilon^{\prime}\leadsto v^{\prime}
,
and for all
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
,
we have
\Omega^{\prime}\vdash_{A}v^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}
.
By
FE:Handle
,

\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\epsilon\leadsto\mathsf{handle}[\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}]~v^{\prime}
.
By
US:Prod
,

\mathcal{U}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\blacklozenge^{\square}\boxplus\mathcal{U}^{\prime\square}\times\blacklozenge^{\blacksquare}
.
By Lemma
7
,
for all
\Omega
such that
\Omega;\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
,
there exists
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
such that

\Omega\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{1}\boxplus\Omega_{2}
and

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\blacksquare}\times\blacklozenge^{\square}
and

\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime\square}\times\blacklozenge^{\blacksquare}
.
By
U:Fst
and
U:Snd
,

\Omega_{1};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{fst}~\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\Omega_{2};\cdot\vdash\mathsf{snd}~\mathit{VP}:\blacklozenge
.
Apply
SV:Handle
.

•

F:Rec
.
Then
\sigma=\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}

and
\tau=\mu(\alpha;u:\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\tau^{\prime};\mathit{VP})

and
\mathcal{U}=\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

and
\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash_{u}\sigma^{\prime}\leadsto\tau^{\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
.
By inversion on
SU:Roll
,

\epsilon=\mathsf{roll}~\epsilon^{\prime}

and
\vdash\epsilon^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]
.
By inversion on
KU:Rec
,

\alpha::\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\sigma^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By Lemma
2
,

\cdot\vdash\sigma^{\prime}[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By Lemma
3
,

\cdot\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\sigma^{\prime}[\mu\alpha.\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha]\leadsto\tau^{\prime\prime};\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
,
and for all
\Psi
such that
\cdot;\Psi\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]

and
\cdot;\Psi,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathsf{Ty}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
,

\cdot;\Psi;\cdot\vdash\tau^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime}[\mathit{VP}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By induction,
there exists
v^{\prime}

such that
\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\epsilon^{\prime}\leadsto v^{\prime}
,
and for all
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime};\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]
,
we have
\Omega\vdash_{A}v^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime\prime}
.
By
FE:Roll
,

\vdash_{\mathit{VP}}\epsilon\leadsto\mathsf{roll}~v^{\prime}
.
By
UT:Corec1
and
U:Subtype
,

\Omega;\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]

for all
\Omega
such that
\Omega;\cdot\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
.
By Lemma
8
,

\cdot;\Omega\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}[\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/t]

and
\cdot;\Omega\vdash\mathit{VP}:\mathcal{U}
.
By part
2
,

\cdot;\Omega;\Upsilon\vdash\tau::\mathsf{Ty}
.
By inversion on
K:Rec
,

\cdot;\Omega,u:\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime};\alpha::\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\vdash\tau^{\prime}::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Thus
\cdot;\Omega;\cdot\vdash\tau^{\prime\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime}[\mathit{VP}/u][\lambda u^{\prime}\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\mu(\alpha;u\!:\!\nu t.\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.\tau^{\prime};u^{\prime})/\alpha]::\mathsf{Ty}
.
Apply
SV:Type-Eq
and
SV:Roll
.

∎
